@@40996449 <h> Gas Flare Commercialisation : Another Solution To Energy Waste ? <p> LAGOS -- Nigeria 's losses from gas flaring has frighteningly climbed to $1billion annually , eleven years before the global campaign to exterminate the practice and safe the environment from pollution . <p> Although the Federal Government claimed that the country had already eliminated 75 per cent of flared gas and was on track to eliminate same next year , previous efforts by the Federal Government to curb incidences of gas flaring have yielded little prospects . <p> Besides , the Tax Appeal Tribunal has held that levies paid for flaring gas under the AGRA are tax deductible . The implication is that oil producing companies can flare as much gas as they want to , and deduct the levies they pay for flaring this gas from their taxable income . <p> But in recognition that flared gas could be harnessed to stimulate economic growth , drive investments and provide jobs in oil producing communities through the utilisation of widely available innovative technologies , the Federal Executive Council ( Nigeria 's cabinet ) has approved the Nigerian Gas Flare Commercialisation Programme ( " NGFCP " ) which @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ ( " Minister " ) on December 13 , 2016 . <p> The policy position of the Federal Government is that gas flaring is unacceptable and the Federal Government has initiated a number of actions to reaffirm its commitment to ending the practice of gas flaring in our oil fields . <p> Specifically , the Federal Government has ratified the Paris Climate Change Agreement , and is a signatory to the Global Gas Flaring Partnership ( GGFR ) principles for global flare-out by 2030 whilst committing to a national flare-out target by year 2020 . <p> The NGFCP is designed as the strategy to implement the policy objectives of the Federal Government for the elimination of gas flares with potentially enormous multiplier and development outcomes for Nigeria . <p> The objective of the NGFCP is to eliminate gas flaring through technically and commercially sustainable gas utilisation projects developed by competent third party investors who will be invited to participate in a competitive and transparent bid process . <p> The commercialisation approach has been considered from legal , technical , economic , commercial and developmental standpoints to attract major investment in @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ 60 year environmental problem in Nigeria . <p> The @@40997711 <h> British PM 's resignation : Lessons for Nigerian leaders <p> On Friday last week British Prime Minister Theresa May declared that she would step aside having failed to come up with a BREXIT agreement to lead her country out of the European Union . In deciding to relinquish office because of her failure to deliver upon a campaign promise and the results of a referendum , she set an example which Nigerian leaders should emulate . Indeed , there are many lessons for Nigerian leaders to learn from the episode . The British Prime Minister said quite correctly that in a democracy if you give people a choice you have the duty to implement what they decide . To her and all principled political leaders it follows logically that if you ca n't deliver , you step aside . Many Nigerian political leaders will pour scorn on her for giving up the comforts , protocol and prestige of Prime Ministership without losing an election . <p> Constitutionally she did n't have to go , it was simply the right thing for her to do . Theresa May is n't the first . On the whole British Prime Ministers @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ full or wait to be disgraced out of office . They react to obvious public disapproval as expressed by opinion-poll ratings , and the disaffection of their appointees and the media . Nigerian leaders on the other hand ignore public opinion and sit-tight no matter their failures . They get carried away by the pomp , pageantry and protocol of office and pay little attention to the substance . <p> ADVERTISEMENT <p> The outgoing British Prime Minister made it very clear as to what leaders are supposed to do . She said that the unique privilege of high office is " to use the platform to give a voice to the voiceless , and to fight the burning injustices that still scar our society " . May 's downfall could never have happened in Nigeria . Firstly , because her Ministers deserted her and resigned on principle . In Nigeria Ministers do n't resign on principle merely because they disagree with their boss , invariably they resign to move upwards not downwards . They resign so as to contest elections for governorship or take up lucrative foreign appointments . If @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ of their leader 's ill-conceived policies or actions he or she would most likely be " finished " . <p> It 's more than likely the various corruption and security apparatus would ensure that they end up in prison where they would have ample time to reflect upon their stupidity ! In Nigeria once appointed as a Minister it 's your duty to defend the indefensible and play the role of " yes man " . Prime Minister May 's speech inadvertently revealed a major reason for her failure . She said that she believed that it was right for her to persevere even when the odds against success seemed high . She now knows better ! In Nigeria there is a long history of leaders pushing through policies which from the onset had little or no chance of success simply because they have no principled opposition from within . Recognising that she had lost the moral battle Prime Minister May went on to say that her successor must seek a way forward , which would involve finding consensus in parliament which she was unable to do . In true @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ consensus . <p> ADVERTISEMENT <p> Nigerian leaders must imbibe a culture which accepts that politics is about managing disagreement , not about bulldozing your way through regardless of the consequences . Although Theresa May 's said that she was advised never to forget that compromise is n't a dirty word and that life depends on it , she could n't bring herself to reach an acceptable compromise . The inability of Nigerian leaders to seek compromise unless faced with violent opposition is legendary . Upon assuming Office , the purpose for which they were elected recedes into the background and campaign promises are n't considered as binding . <p> Another lesson for Nigeria is that similar to our situation Theresa May said her country is a union , not just a family of four nations , but a union of people . She further said " our politics may be under strain , but there is so much that is good about the is country . So much to be proud of . So much to be optimistic about " . However , in contrast to Britain the things that @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ do with good governance , while those things which make us ashamed tend to revolve around our politics . In conclusion May said " I will shortly leave the job that it has been the honour of my life to hold . I do so with no ill will , but with enormous and enduring gratitude to have had the opportunity to serve the country that I love . Nigerian leaders are renowned for their ill-will against their successors . <p> When outlining her achievements Prime Minister May referred to protecting the environment , eliminating plastic waste , tackling climate change and improving air quality . In contrast Nigerian leaders focus their attention on purely economic issues forgetting that there are so many other factors that can improve the quality of life even in depressed insecure environments . There are so many lessons about principled leadership that Nigerians can still learn from our former colonial masters .

Our founding fathers , Mr Herbert Macauley , Dr Nnamdi Azikiwe , Chief Obafemi @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ , Alhaji Abubakar Tafawa Balewa , Malam Aminu Kano , Chief J.S. @@41004646 <h> World Hunger Day : Nigerian farmers appeal for government support <h> Related News <p> Every day , many across the globe struggle to feed themselves and their children . <p> According to the World Food Programme , in a world where enough food is produced to feed everyone , 821 million people -- one in nine of the world population -- still go to bed on an empty stomach each night . <p> Even more -- one in three -- suffer from some form of malnutrition . <p> World Hunger day is celebrated on May 28 every year . It was launched by The Hunger Project in 2011 . <p> It is aimed to inspire people all over the world to show their solidarity and support for the women , men and children who are ending their hunger and poverty . <p> In a bid to mark this day , PREMIUM TIMES spoke with some farmers on how they can support the need for more food to be available for the hungry . <p> Many of the farmers interviewed advised President Muhammadu Buhari to assist the agricultural sector through funding and sound policies . <h> Voices <p> A @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ failed to invest substantially in the small scale farmers who provide food for the nation . <p> She accused the government of investing in the ' established ' farmers who export their produce to other countries . <p> Ms Isiaya also said farmers in Nigeria are faced with the challenge of inadequate information from the government . <p> " We did not hear of climate change ; we did not know the rains will come early and leave early . Most farmers have cultivated and will end up losing their produce , " she said . <p> She said the produce from the small scale farmers is very little because " they lack inputs from the government . " <p> " They sell at cost price and are left with little or nothing for the next farming season , " she said . <p> The Managing Director , A&amp;D Integrated Farms , Abdullahi Bello , also urged President Buhari to give more priority to agriculture " to ensure the eradication of poverty and hunger in the country . " <p> He said that federal , states and local governments need @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ and exportation . <p> " We know that this administration has been supporting the sector from the beginning of the tenure , and we are happy that they are continuing , which will make them build more on what they have done . <p> " They should also look out for where there are loopholes so that they can make necessary adjustments where needed . <p> " We are all hungry in this country because many things they have been saying about agriculture have not been done . We need to be self-sufficient by feeding ourselves comfortably and export for foreign exchange , ' ' he said . <p> Mr Bello said that some commodities were given ' more priority ' than others because of " the political and personal interest of some people . " <p> He said all the commodities produced locally should be given the needed priority . <p> " The small-holder farmers that produce more than 70 per cent of what we consume in this country need to be supported as well . <p> " They need support in terms of land , inputs , fertilisers @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ perform and remain in the sector . " <p> Another farmer , Donald Akule , said hunger is the root cause of poverty . <p> He said agriculture should be enhanced so that youth , women and retirees can participate fully . <p> " Agribusiness has become interesting to the populace , " he said . <p> Mr Akule called for sensitisation of Nigerians on the need to contribute to the fight against hunger . <h> Global Blight <p> According to the National Research Council , world hunger refers to hunger aggregated to the global level . Related terms include food insecurity and malnutrition . <p> Food insecurity refers to limited or unreliable access to foods that are safe and nutritionally adequate . <p> Malnutrition , which is an offshoot , is a condition resulting from insufficient intake of biologically necessary nutrients . <p> Although malnutrition includes both overnutrition and undernutrition , the focus for global hunger is undernutrition . <p> The vast majority of hungry people live in lower-middle-income regions , which saw a 42 per cent reduction in the prevalence of undernourished people between 1990 -- 92 and @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ are the most visible victims of undernutrition . <p> The World Health Organisation estimates that undernutrition is a cause of 3*1 million child deaths annually or 45 per cent of all child deaths in 2011 . <p> Undernutrition magnifies the effect of every disease , including measles and malaria . <p> The estimated proportions of deaths in which undernutrition is an underlying cause are roughly similar for diarrhoea ( 61 per cent ) , malaria ( 57 per cent ) , pneumonia ( 52 per cent ) , and measles ( 45 per cent ) . <p> Undernutrition can also be caused by diseases , such as those that cause diarrhoea , by reducing the body 's ability to convert food into usable nutrients . <p> For now , nations such as Nigeria will have to continue churning out relevant policies and strengthening those in place to reduce the number of persons who go hungry daily . <h> NEVER MISS A THING AGAIN ! The FGN will work to grant open access to all pipelines and other essential midstream infrastructure . With respect to pricing of gas for the domestic market , which is largely controlled by the FGN under a transitional @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ a limited period until a sufficient gas market is established . The policy objective is to move to market-led wholesale gas pricing without gas price regulation , except where there are natural monopolies . <p> The FGN included gas flare reduction as a key national greenhouse gas emissions mitigation in its Nationally Determined Contributions ( NDC ) under the United Nations Framework Convention on Climate Change ( UNFCCC ) . <p> Premised on the foregoing , the policy position of His Excellency , President Muhammad Buhari is that gas flaring is totally unacceptable . In this regard , the FGN initiated a number of actions to reaffirm its commitment to ending the practice of gas flaring in our oil fields . Specifically , His Excellency , President Muhammad Buhari ratified the Paris Climate Change Agreement , and the FGN is a signatory to the Global Gas Flaring Partnership ( GGFR ) principles for global flare-out by 2030 whilst committing to a national flare-out target by year 2020 . <p> Furthermore , in recognition that flared gas could be harnessed to stimulate economic growth , drive investments and provide jobs in @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ of widely available innovative technologies , the Federal Executive Council in June 2016 , approved the Nigerian Gas Flare Commercialisation Programme ( NGFCP ) . The programme was launched by the Honorable minister of state on December 13 , 2016 . <p> The design of the NGFCP according to our development partners is an innovative , robust and scalable approach to gas flare reduction -- a " game changer " ( first of a kind ) consistent with the climate change action plans anticipated in the Paris Climate Change Accords which could be replicable in many other gas flaring countries around the World with Nigeria setting the pace . <p> Please accept the assurances of the Honourable Minister of State 's best regards . 
@@41005411 <p> The World Bank 's Board of Executive Directors today approved $200 million Renewable Energy Guarantees Program ( REGREP ) to mobilize International Development Association ( IDA ) guarantees under a Multi-phased Programmatic Approach ( MPA ) . The program will support the Government of Ethiopia 's ongoing power sector reforms and leverage private sector financing for renewable energy generation . <p> Ethiopia has vast and largely unharnessed clean energy resources . Today , Ethiopia is one of the few countries in the world which generates almost all the electricity from renewable resources , mostly hydropower , which also makes it vulnerable to climate change . The country also has vast and mainly untapped solar , wind , and geothermal energy resources . This program supports diversification from hydropower and towards modern renewables -- in solar and wind power . REGREP will support the development of over 1,000 MW of greenfield solar and wind energy Independent Power Producer ( IPP ) projects in Ethiopia , including the World Bank Group Scaling Solar initiative . <p> " REGREP comes at this critical juncture and signals the Government 's commitment to comprehensive power sector reforms and a private sector led renewable @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ of the largest in Sub-Saharan Africa , " said Rahul Kitchlu , Senior Energy Specialist at the WorldBank . <p> The Ethiopia REGREP approved today marks the first IDA guarantees under deployment in Ethiopia . Enabled by the 2018 Public-Private Partnership Proclamation , this program reflects a new way of doing business in the energy sector in Ethiopia -- transitioning from continued public-financing towards private sector led competitively tendered procurement of new renewable generation capacity . <p> The program is fully aligned with the World Bank 's Country Partnership framework for FY18 -- FY22 . It will foster structural transformation for growth by enhancing private sector financing of infrastructure projects ; build resilience and inclusiveness by increasing supply of electricity sustainably and help to manage the impact of climate change through diversification of energy sources . <p> " With the support of the World Bank Group , this program will create a platform for much-needed private sector participation in the crucial energy sector by lowering the risks of investing in Ethiopia , " said Carolyn Turk , World Bank Country Director for Ethiopia , Sudan and South Sudan " The @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ private sector investment , " she added . <p> In line with the Maximizing Financing for Development ( MFD ) approach , the World Bank Group 's support to Ethiopia 's IPP program follows a sequenced approach under which financing and technical assistance for policy reforms have unlocked significant opportunities for private sector participation in the power sector . The Energy Sector Management Assistance Program ( ESMAP ) provided support in terms of resource mapping and validation , as well as technical studies to identify areas for wind development . <p> To maximize the effectiveness of development results and make the environment for private sector investments more conducive , in addition to IDA guarantees , complementary instruments of the International Finance Corporation ( IFC ) and Multilateral Investment Guarantee Agency ( MIGA ) will also be made available . 
@@41005657 <h> Green Financing in Nigeria <p> The first green bond in Nigeria was issued in 2017 . Since then , the Nigerian green bond market has continued to gain considerable traction . This year alone , we have seen North South Power Company ( " NSP " ) Limited , a pan African power generation company , issue its first N8.5billion 15-year 15.6 percent Series 1 Guaranteed Fixed Rate Senior Green Infrastructure Bonds , due 2034 ; through NSP-SPV PowerCorp Plc -- a special purpose vehicle for raising funds from the capital market to finance capital expenditure and optimize the capital structure of NSP . This is the first series of a N50billion senior green infrastructure bond and it was oversubscribed by 60 percent . Even more recently , Access Bank Plc listed its N15billion 5-year 15.5 percent Fixed Rate Senior Unsecured Green Bond , on the FMDQ OTC Securities Exchange and the Nigerian Stock Exchange ( NSE ) , due in 2024 . <p> Defining Green Bonds A green bond is any type of debt instrument , the proceeds of which would be exclusively applied to finance or re-finance in part or in full , new and/or existing @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ as green projects . <p> As a viable alternative to traditional financing mechanisms , green bonds offer easy access to a large and diverse funding pool , providing a source of low-cost and much-needed capital to finance infrastructure projects and set up green funding programs . The cornerstone of a green bond is the utilisation of the proceeds of the bond for green projects , including other related and supporting expenditures . <p> A person desirous of issuing green bonds in Nigeria is required to comply with the Securities and Exchange Commission 's ( " SEC " ) Green Bonds Issuance Rules ( " the Green Bonds Rules " ) together with SEC 's new listing rules , which codify the criteria and approval process of green bonds in Nigeria . <p> Interestingly , the Green Bonds Rules align with the International Capital Market Association 's Green Bond Principles ( " the GBP " ) . In the main , the GBP are voluntary process guidelines for issuing green bonds . The GBP are intended for use by market participants and are designed to drive the provision of information needed @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ focus on the use of proceeds of green bonds , the GBP recommend transparency , disclosure , and reporting as way of promoting integrity in the green bond market . <p> Qualifying as Green Projects As previously mentioned , the distinguishing element in green bonds , is the utilization of its proceeds on green projects . To qualify as a green project under the Green Bonds Rules , an investment must fall into one or more of the following : renewable and sustainable energy , clean transportation , sustainable water management , climate change adaptation , energy efficiency , sustainable waste management , sustainable land use , biodiversity conservation , green buildings ( commercial real estate development ) and any other categories as may be approved by SEC from time to time . <p> Conditions for Approval of Green Bonds by SEC <p> In addition to the general registration requirements for debt issuances as stated in the Rules and Regulations of SEC for States , Local Governments , Government , Corporate and Supranational agencies , an Issuer of a Green Bond is required to file : <p> * A letter @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ projects that qualify as green projects or assets in line with the Green Bonds Rules ; <p> * A feasibility Study and Report stating clearly , the measurable benefits of the proposed green project(s) or asset(s) such as greenhouse gas reduction , reduction of water use and reduction of harmful emissions ; <p> * A prospectus which shall include project categories , project selection criteria , decision-making procedures , environmental benefits , use and management of the proceeds . <p> * An independent assessment or certification issued by a professional certification authority or person approved or recognized by SEC ; and * Any other documents that may be required by SEC . <p> UTILIZATION AND MANAGEMENT OF PROCEEDS Again , the cornerstone of a green bond is the utilization of its proceeds . On this , the Green Bonds Rules provides that : <p> * The net proceeds shall only be utilized for the purpose stated in the approved offer documents and shall be tracked as stated in the approved internal policy of the Issuer which shall be disclosed in the offer documents . <p> * An escrow account shall @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ . <p> * The proceeds shall be domiciled with the Custodian and the Trustees shall ensure that the proceeds are used for the purpose stated in the prospectus . <p> * The Issuer and the Trustees shall be the signatories to the escrow account . <p> * The Issuer shall invest proceeds in green projects within the given timeframe prescribed in the prospectus . <p> * Unallocated proceeds shall be invested by the Trustees in money market instruments with investment grade rating that do not include greenhouse gas intensive projects which are inconsistent with the delivery of a low carbon and climate resilient economy . <p> Issuing Green Bonds in Nigeria Giving the need for accountability and transparency in the utilization of green bond proceeds , the Green Bonds Rules require the Issuer to provide to SEC and the Stock Exchange ( where listed ) , at least annually , a Green Bond Report containing the list of the project(s) and asset(s) to which proceeds have been allocated for the duration of the bond . <p> The reporting process and authority must be documented and maintained as part of the @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ here is that transparency is of particular value in communicating the expected impact of projects . <p> Accordingly , the Green Bond Report should include a brief description of the project(s) and the amounts disbursed , including ( where possible ) the percentage of proceeds that have been allocated to different eligible sectors and project types and to financing and refinancing . In any case , where confidentiality agreements or competition considerations limit the amount of details that can be disclosed , the information may be presented in generic terms . <p> Further , the report should state the expected impact of the project(s) and asset(s) , qualitative performance indicators and , where feasible , quantitative performance measures of the impact of the project(s) . The report should further state the methodology and underlying assumptions used to prepare performance indicators and metrics shall be disclosed . <p> There is also a requirement on the Issuer to publish an assessment report issued by an independent professional assessment or certification agency on its website or other media , conduct and report annual follow-up assessments of the green project(s) and associated environmental benefits @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ its annual report and on its website or other media , a copy of which should be filed with SEC . <p> REFINANCING EXISTING GREEN ASSETS It is quite likely that an Issuer intends to apply a portion of the proceeds of the issued green bonds , towards refinancing existing green project(s) or assets . In such a case , the Issuer is required to clearly provide in the offer document , the details of the portfolio or asset(s) or project(s) which are identified for such refinancing , and to the extent relevant , the expected look-back period for refinanced project(s) . <p> CONCLUSION Whilst the Green Bond market is far from mature , this year does offers some brighter prospects , given the regulatory and participatory momentum . @@41005844 <h> Celebrating Children 's Day <p> On Monday , Nigerian kids celebrated Children 's Day . Since they are a part of every society throughout the world , it is simply right to set aside one day every year to celebrate children . Globally , the idea of setting aside a special day for children around the world was originally mooted by Rubab Mansoor , a child in the 8th grade and was first proclaimed by the World Conference for the Well-being of Children in 1925 before it was given universal prominence in 1954 <p> Children 's Day was established as a day of worldwide fraternity and understanding among children . Countries were encouraged to pick their own days to celebrate these special gifts of God who are often referred to as leaders of tomorrow , as well as promote mutual exchange and understanding among them , and also initiate beneficial activities that promote their welfare . For us here in Nigeria , Children 's Day is celebrated annually on May 27 . <p> Every international children 's day comes bearing its own theme for celebration . The theme , which is usually picked to reflect trending issues in @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ guides each government on what the celebration should be like . For instance , last year , the United Nations picked #GoBlue , a theme informed by the need to get world leaders to do something urgent to stop climate change and also to remind them of the need to commit to fulfilling the rights of every child and acknowledge that these rights are non-negotiable . The #GoBlue theme prompted many protests around the world , especially in Europe , with children carrying placards with inscriptions suggesting an end to every human activity that endangers the earth . <p> For the Nigerian child , celebrating this day on Monday was merely about the yearly routine which entails assembling a few schools in state capitals in all the states of the federation and the Federal Capital Territory for a marching parade . Aside this , other activities might just be what parents had planned to celebrate their children , especially since the day is a holiday for them . <p> Apart from paying lip service to the theme of this year 's Children 's Day celebration , " Drug Abuse among @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ no clear cut plan to help government focus on the problems that bedevil Nigerian children like it is done in some other countries . In the United Kingdom , for instance , this year 's theme is focussing on tacking childhood obesity and the physical and mental health problems that accompany it . That is what the government and parents are worried about , and certainly , blueprint must have been mapped out to address the issue . <p> Yet the Nigerian child is one of the most endangered in the world . They are the ones who have been rated as the most out-of-school children the world over . They are among the hungriest children in the world in view of the position of the country on the poverty index . Nigerian children are rated top among children that might die before their 5th birthday from common diseases like malaria , meningitis , malnutrition and cholera . They are the ones with little or no chance of getting their dream jobs because their parents are not able to bribe anyone on their behalf . Nigerian children are with little @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ as a career because the system is designed in such a way that you must be educated in a particular field to be employable , while little or no attention is paid to talent and interest . <p> As much as we agree as appropriate what our celebration is about up until now , we wish to suggest that the time has come for the Nigerian government to revisit the real purpose for which the day was set up . This is a day for government to review laws that protect the rights of children and , where necessary , improve on them to meet the age and time in which children are born to these days . <p> In our view , it would not be out of place also for us as a country to begin to take action on the theme of each year 's celebration . If that were the case before now , this year 's theme would have been one that would celebrate Nigerian children who have been abducted by terrorist from their school and are now in captivity , with a focus on @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ and reunited with their families and friends . Fresh on our mind is Leah Sharibu who recently celebrated her 16 birthday in captivity . <p> It is high time the Nigerian government worked really hard at initiating beneficial activities that promote the welfare of Nigerian children to ensure our future as a nation . 
@@41006082 <h> Abuja : Green Spaces And The Moves To Combat Climate Change <p> Climate change may be simply understood as change in global or regional climate patterns . In particular a change apparent from the mid to late 20th century onwards and attributed largely to the increased levels of atmospheric carbon dioxide produced by the use of fossil fuels . It is also known as global warming , caused by human activity , as opposed to changes in climate that may have resulted as part of earth 's natural processes , especially in the context of environmental policies . <p> The term climate change was proposed by World Meteorological Organisation ( WMO ) in 1966 to encompass all forms of climatic variability on time-scales longer than 10 years , regardless of cause . <p> These changes may also be occasioned by high concentration of greenhouse gases in the atmosphere . <p> The federal capital territory Abuja is said to have a population of over 1,406,239 as of the 2017 records . According to the world urbanisation prospects population review , the population of Abuja is estimated to stand at 2,892,000 in 2018 . This indicates a huge increase and @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ the city has recorded the highest temperature of 40 ? C , which is about 103 ? F and the lowest temperature of 4 ? C which is equivalent of 39 ? F in January . <p> The solution to global warming according to experts is natural with the open space strategy . Open spaces and previously developed land ( PDL ) can and will make a valuable contribution to helping both to reduce concentrations of greenhouse gases in the atmosphere and to help adapt effectively to the impacts of change Urban greening is a cost effective way of moderating harsh climates at a local level . Trees and vegetation have a natural cooling effect as they provide shade , potentially reducing surface temperature , by 5 ? C , to 20 ? C. <p> The functionality of open green spaces becomes increasingly important <p> when viewed against the backdrop of a changing climate . This is the basis of the Abuja Master plan , which provides green spaces for trees and vegetation . <p> In a review of the FCT Master plan , a Professor of urban and design @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ titled ' symptoms of distress ' said that those saddled with the responsibility of developing the city have not followed over the years . <p> Some of the symptoms of distress include distortion to land use , multiplication of pocket layouts , sector district organisation , deviations in the location of key government buildings , loss of green areas , development of unregulated settlements and development along Abuja-Keffi road . <p> Prof Gyuse said the failure of the Abuja Master Plan depends on understanding and implementing the residential components , adding that each sector was conceived as a mini city of 100,000 to 250,000 inhabitants . <p> He noted that many of the major government institutional buildings are not located where planned such as the Presidential Villa . " The Aso Rock was supposed to be the presidential palace , located on the mall near the National Mosque . It was relocated in the main park that was to surround the legislative arm of government . Visible forms and identifying elements of our capital city are now lost in the forest of ' development ' ' ' he said . @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ is evident in the comparison between the approved international planning associates ( IPA ) plan and the actual development on ground . He revealed that at the monumental core in the central area was to be a national square as the anchor of two radial streets with a national mall that linked to the national assembly and other monumental symbols of government . <p> The presidential palace surrounded by presidential gardens was to be in this area but that is not the case . In reality , the concept of national mall where all the ministries and many government offices are located does not exist . <p> He further noted that beyond the national sports centre was to be park way from the airport intended to be a green area that welcomes visitors but this green area ( parkway ) is now lined with buildings , estates and institutions . <p> The Abuja Master Plan envisaged that the natural watercourses in the areas where possible . Following the distortion of the Abuja Master Plan , Residents of the Federal Capital Territory ( FCT ) under the aegis of concerned Abuja @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ , Malam Muhammad Musa Bello to declare a state of emergency on the lingering abuse of the abuse of Abuja master plan . <p> In a letter to the minister , which was signed by Chuks Akamadu and made available to LEADERSHIP , the group noted a brazen distortion of the Abuja Master plan right from the Federal Capital City to satellite towns and suburbs . <p> The residents pointed out that the development of adequate recreational and green areas within the FCT is a key feature of the master plan , but does not reflect the reality on ground . The letter cited the sorry state of Kubwa that does not boast of any meaningful recreational facility or designated green areas as reflected in the actual plan and allays fears that such fate may befall other satellite towns if the minister does not declare a state of emergency on the abuse of the Master plan . <p> While noting that green areas have been converted to residential or commercial buildings especially in phase 3 and FO1 layout the group pointed out that with the federal government 's avowed commitment @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ the green areas of Abuja for the benefit of the residents . <p> A resident of Kubwa , Mrs. Fatima Ahmed , who spoke to LEADERSHIP , said that some personalities connive with their cohorts in government to convert the green areas to either residential or commercial buildings . <p> Ahmed stated that green areas in phase 3 , apart from being overtaken with shanties , have also been clandestinely converted to other purposes . " Last year in October , officials of the Federal Capital Development Authority ( FCDA ) destroyed some structures here and left some belonging to their friends , " she said . <p> Furthermore , a visit to the green area allotted to FO1 layout revealed that the actual green area on ground does not reflect the original plan as contained in the Abuja Master plan.It is gathered that the recommendations of the FCT inter directorate committed set up to resolve the issue of land allocations in Kubwa , if implemented would further destroy the Abuja Master Plan . <p> A source told LEADERSHIP that the committee , whose report is before the minister for @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ in FO1 layout be allocated to people for residential purposes thereby undermining the dictates of the original plan of the town . <p> Efforts to reach the director , FCT Parks and Garden , Alhaji Ali Ukele to speak on the arbitrary conversion of open spaces ended in futility as he neither picked nor returned calls made to him . However , only recently , the minister of the Federal Capital Territory Administration ( FCTA ) , Malam Muhammad Musa Bello disclosed that plans were on for a holistic review of the 38-year master plan of Abuja to ensure that some of the wrongs perpetuated in the past were corrected . He said that the administration was jealously guarding the implementation of the master plan . 
@@41007207 <h> Mozambique seeks $3.2 billion to recover from cyclones <p> JOHANNESBURG ( AP ) -- Mozambique says it needs $3.2 billion to recover from a pair of powerful tropical cyclones that ripped into the southern African nation earlier this year and left hundreds dead , raising alarm about the effects of climate change on coastal nations . <p> That 's according to a government assessment supported by the World Bank , United Nations and European Union for a global pledging conference that begins on Friday . <p> Some aid workers fear the worst is yet to come . The storms wiped out crops on the eve of harvest in one of the world 's least developed countries , and many people must rely on food aid well into next year . More than 1.3 million people are said to require emergency food assistance . <p> Cyclones Idai and Kenneth killed more than 650 people in Mozambique , plus hundreds of others in Zimbabwe and Malawi . A final death toll may never be known as some bodies were washed away . <p> It was the first time in recorded history that two tropical cyclones struck Mozambique in a single @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ recorded . The country 's 2,400-kilometer ( 1,500-mile ) Indian Ocean coastline is one of the world 's most vulnerable to global warming . <p> The cyclones will worsen poverty in the region and recovery will be " extremely challenging , " the government assessment says . <p> " It 's getting worse with time , steadily , " Mahmoud Shabeeb , spokesman with the aid organization CARE , told The Associated Press , saying the next harvest season is n't until March of next year . " If people receive assistance , they eat . If not , they do n't eat . " <p> Cyclone Idai in mid-March brought widespread flooding that created an " inland ocean " and left panicked residents clinging to rooftops and trees for days across central Mozambique , the country 's breadbasket . In the region 's main city of Beira , population roughly 500,000 , many rooftops were peeled away . <p> Six weeks later Kenneth arrived in the northernmost province of Cabo Delgado , shocking residents who had never experienced such a powerful storm . A long swathe of coastal communities @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ impossible to reach . <p> " The sound was like a pack of angry wolves , " Ahmad Baroudi , a spokesman with the aid group Save the Children , told the AP after visiting devastated Ibo island and hearing accounts of the storm . Residents told him how they had struggled to put one foot in front of the other in the hurricane-force winds , and how everyone -- even those not thought to be religious -- were praying . <p> The cyclones ' effects on children have been widespread , Baroudi said . Fewer are going to school . Some are exposed to abuse and neglect . Some must work or engage in " survival sex . " Many are drinking unsafe water . Livelihoods have been destroyed .

@@41013244 <p> Nepali government has removed minimum threshold for most types of foreign assistance as the Himalayan country aims to receive maximum aid either in the form of grant or loan to spearhead its development activities . <p> Shreekrishna Nepal , chief of international cooperation coordination division at the Nepal 's Finance Ministry told Xinhua on Friday that the minimum threshold was removed as per the government 's aim of maximising finances . <p> The government is maximising finances to upgrade the country to developing country from least developed status at first and stated goal of transforming the country into middle income country by 2030 . <p> Loading ... <p> " Instead of telling the donors ' No ' to their small grant or loan , the policy aims to streamline small assistance into pool fund to use them into single large project , ' ' Nepal said . <p> " Question also arises whether to accept aid of 4.5 million U.S. dollar if we keep threshold at five million U.S. dollar for grant , ' ' said the official . <p> According to a new Development Cooperation Policy released by Nepal 's Finance Ministry earlier , minimum threshold set @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ <p> Such threshold has been maintained only for receiving concessional loan that Nepal has been receiving from multilateral development partners such as the World Bank and the Asian Development Bank . <p> The new Development Cooperation Policy which replaced the old one introduced in 2014 was released after the Nepal 's cabinet approved it . <p> As per the old policy , minimum threshold for receiving grant was fixed at 5 million U.S. dollar per project . <p> The new policy removed this threshold but it stated that the size of grant would be made larger as far as possible through other means . <p> " The donors will be encouraged to contribute small grant to a pool fund created after combining contribution from several donors , ' ' the new policy states . <p> The Nepali government aims to spend grant in the areas of environment protection to tackle climate change , sustainable development efforts and rural infrastructure . <p> The new policy has kept the threshold unchanged at 10 million U.S. dollar per project for the concessional loan , in which interest rate remains very low . <p> @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ hydropower , renewable energy , road , bridge , irrigation , airport , railway , dry ports , urban infrastructure , agriculture and tourism infrastructure , the policy states . <p> The new policy also removed the threshold for other loans including commercial loans which was maintained at 20 million U.S. dollar per project in the earlier policy . <p> The Himalayan country , which has been receiving loans from foreign Export-Import Banks including such banks from both China and India sometime ago , has sought to use such loans in the areas of hydropower , highway , railway , airport , dry port and urban infrastructure projects . <p> The policy has , for the first time , opened the door for receiving foreign assistance under Engineering , Procurement , Construction and Financing ( EPCF ) modality where the developer of the project gets involved in all tasks including financing . <p> A number of Nepali private sector hydropower developers have signed agreements with Chinese companies to develop the projects under EPCF modality.
